### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/ingestion/extraction_strategies/cached_chunk_extraction.py:366
**_merge keys edges on GraphRelationship.type, which is always the constant "RELATES"**

- **Impact:** GraphExtraction._relations_to_relationships always sets type="RELATES" (graph_extraction.py:1203) and puts the real predicate in properties['rel_type'], so the middle element of the key is a constant and the key degenerates to (start, end). If an unchanged chunk contributes 'Alice WORKS_AT Acme' (cached part) and a changed chunk contributes 'Alice FOUNDED Acme' (fresh part), _merge collapses them: {**current.properties, **rel.properties} makes the fresh FOUNDED win and the WORKS_AT fact is destroyed, while its source_chunk_ids are unioned onto the wrong predicate. The docstring at line 325 explicitly claims this mirrors _aggregate_relations, but _aggregate_relations (graph_extraction.py:1101) keys on (source, type, target) where type IS the predicate, so the two paths are not equivalent.
- **Code:** `for rel in part.relationships: key = (rel.start_node_id, rel.type, rel.end_node_id) current = rels_by_key.get(key) if current is None: rels_by_key[key] = rel else: merged = _union_sources(current.properties or {}, rel.properties or {})`
- **Fix:** Use (rel.start_node_id, rel.properties.get('rel_type', rel.type), rel.end_node_id) as the merge key, and correct the docstring claim about equivalence with _aggregate_relations.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/ingestion/extraction_strategies/cached_chunk_extraction.py:347
**_merge lets a freshly extracted empty description overwrite a cached non-empty one**

- **Impact:** The cached branch is careful to emit only non-empty fields precisely because of SET n += props (see the comment at line 237: 'Only emit non-empty fields - SET n += props would null-out existing values otherwise'). The fresh branch is not: _entities_to_nodes (graph_extraction.py:1148) unconditionally writes "description": ent.description, and a step-1 GLiNER entity that survives a step-2 failure has description "". Entity 'Paris' mentioned in both an unchanged chunk (cached props description='Capital of France') and a changed chunk whose step 2 failed (fresh props description='') merges to description='' because the fresh part is appended last, and upsert then writes the empty string over the stored description. Full re-extraction never does this: _aggregate_entities (graph_extraction.py:1064) keeps the longer description.
- **Code:** `def _union_sources(old_props: dict[str, Any], new_props: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]: merged = {**old_props, **new_props} old_src = old_props.get("source_chunk_ids") or []`
- **Fix:** In _union_sources, skip keys in new_props whose value is falsy when old_props has a non-empty value, or reuse the longer-description rule from _aggregate_entities so the cached and non-cached merge semantics actually match.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/ingestion/extraction_strategies/graph_extraction.py:1007
**Relation rel_type is never normalized, so off-case LLM labels are silently pruned**

- **Impact:** Entity types go through label_for_type() (line 980), which normalizes case and separators against the ontology. Relation types get only .strip(). The prompt asks for UPPER_SNAKE_CASE but nothing enforces it. With an ontology declaring LOCATED_IN, an LLM that answers {"type": "located_in"} produces an ExtractedRelation with rel_type='located_in'; IngestionPipeline._prune (ingestion/pipeline.py:574) does allowed_rels.get(rel_label) as an exact, case-sensitive dict lookup, finds nothing, and drops the edge with no warning (pattern_mismatches only records declared-but-mispatterned labels). Independently, rel_props_by_label.get(rel_type, {}) at line 1022 misses too, so every declared relation attribute for that edge is silently skipped rather than coerced. The module's own comment at graph_extraction.py:72-77 states rel_type is compared case-sensitively downstream, which is exactly why the missing normalization matters.
- **Code:** `rel_type = str(item.get("type", "")).strip() if not source or not target or not rel_type: continue ... declared_rel = rel_props_by_label.get(rel_type, {})`
- **Fix:** Add a _normalize_rel_label() that upper-snake-cases the raw type and, when an ontology is present, resolves it against ontology.relations labels the way label_for_type resolves entity labels; apply it before both the rel_props_by_label lookup and ExtractedRelation construction.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/ingestion/extraction_strategies/graph_extraction.py:1074
**Span dicts are aliased across entities, so aggregation self-extends a shared offset list**

- **Impact:** _merge_step1_metadata (line 914) does ent.spans = s1_spans, assigning the step-1 entity's live dict object rather than a copy, so if step 2 returns the same entity twice in one chunk ('Paris' and 'paris', which normalize to the same key) both verified entities hold the identical dict. In _aggregate_entities the first copy is made with _optional_extras, which does dict(spans) (line 442) - a shallow copy whose inner lists are still the step-1 lists. Merging the second occurrence then evaluates existing_spans.setdefault(cid, []) and offsets as the SAME list object, so .extend(offsets) extends a list with itself: [{'start':0,'end':5}] becomes [{'start':0,'end':5},{'start':0,'end':5}]. The duplicated offsets are written into node properties['spans'] by _entities_to_nodes, and the step-1 entity object is mutated in place as a side effect. CompositeExtractor._merge_spans (entity_extractors.py:1312) gets this right by checking 'if sp not in bucket' before appending.
- **Code:** `ent_spans = getattr(ent, "spans", None) or {} if ent_spans: existing_spans = getattr(existing, "spans", None) or {} for chunk_id, offsets in ent_spans.items(): existing_spans.setdefault(chunk_id, []).extend(offsets) existing.spans = existing_spans # type: ignore[attr-defined]`
- **Fix:** Deep-copy in _optional_extras ({k: list(v) for k, v in spans.items()}) and in _merge_step1_metadata, and dedupe offsets on merge the way CompositeExtractor._merge_spans does.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · resource · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/ingestion/extraction_strategies/coref_resolvers.py:104
**Failed fastcoref load leaves the global transformers monkeypatch permanently applied**

- **Impact:** The restore on line 107 is unguarded. If LingMessCoref(self._model_name) raises for any reason after the patch is installed - a HuggingFace download failure, OOM, a corrupt cache - control jumps to the except block with transformers.LongformerModel._sdpa_can_dispatch still replaced by the lambda, for the lifetime of the process. Every other Longformer user in the same process silently loses SDPA dispatch and runs the slower attention path. Worse, the comment claims 'Fallback without patching' but the retry on line 111 runs with the patch still applied, so the comment describes behaviour the code does not have, and if that retry also raises the process is left permanently patched.
- **Code:** `transformers.LongformerModel._sdpa_can_dispatch = lambda self, *a, **k: False self._model = LingMessCoref(self._model_name) if _orig_sdpa is not None: transformers.LongformerModel._sdpa_can_dispatch = _orig_sdpa except Exception: # Fallback without patching (SDPA dispatch override not supported) log`
- **Fix:** Wrap the patch in try/finally so the restore always runs, then do the fallback load outside that block; correct or delete the 'Fallback without patching' comment.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · resource · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/ingestion/extraction_strategies/coref_resolvers.py:86
**FastCorefResolver has no shared model cache or load lock, unlike both sibling extractors**

- **Impact:** _load_model is reached only from _resolve_sync, which runs under asyncio.to_thread (line 172). Two threads that both see self._model is None construct two LingMessCoref instances, and the loser's several-hundred-MB Longformer stays resident until GC. Across documents it is worse: every GraphExtraction is constructed with its own coref_resolver, so N concurrently ingested documents hold N copies. Both sibling extractors in this package solved exactly this and documented it - GLiNERExtractor._MODEL_CACHE plus _CACHE_LOCK with a measured '~395 MB per copy ... ~11.6 GB for 30 concurrent documents' note (entity_extractors.py:644-660), and SpacyExtractor._MODEL_CACHE which says it 'Mirrors GLiNERExtractor._MODEL_CACHE for the same reason' (entity_extractors.py:1070). This file is the odd one out.
- **Code:** `def _load_model(self) -> Any: if self._model is not None: return self._model try: from fastcoref import LingMessCoref # noqa: F811`
- **Fix:** Add a ClassVar _MODEL_CACHE keyed on model_name plus a ClassVar threading.Lock with the same double-checked-locking shape as GLiNERExtractor._get_shared_model.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · security · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/storage/graph_store.py:134
**Index-creation path interpolates an unsanitised property name into a backticked Cypher identifier**

- **Impact:** `_ensure_label_id_index(label, *extra)` is the one place in the whole store that builds a backtick-quoted identifier WITHOUT `sanitize_cypher_label`. Its only `extra` caller is `reconcile_keyed_identity` (line 424): `await self._ensure_label_id_index(label, signed_key)` — and the very next two lines, 425-426, do `safe_key = sanitize_cypher_label(signed_key)` for the same value. So a signed key of `k` + backtick + `) ON (n.id) //` reaches line 134 raw, closes the identifier and injects into `CREATE INDEX`, while the identical value is sanitised two lines later. Today the only reachable source of `signed_key` is a mapping property name that `core/tables.py::_check_identifier` restricts to `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*`, so it is not exploitable end-to-end — but the sanitiser is the documented single choke point for this surface (25 lines, ~45 call sites, every other one routed through it) and this is the one hole. Any future `extra` caller, or a relaxation of `_check_identifier`, makes it live.
- **Code:** `target = prop if prop in self._LABEL_INDEXED_PROPERTIES else f"`{prop}`" try: await self._conn.query(f"CREATE INDEX FOR (n:`{safe_label}`) ON (n.{target})")`
- **Fix:** `target = prop if prop in self._LABEL_INDEXED_PROPERTIES else f"`{sanitize_cypher_label(prop)}`"`, matching lines 426, 1823 and 1907.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/ingestion/chunking_strategies/sentence_token_cap.py:105
**SentenceTokenCapChunking emits the entire document as one chunk when it has no sentence punctuation**

- **Impact:** `_SENTENCE_END = re.compile(r"(?<=[.!?])\s+")` only splits after `.`/`!`/`?`. Feed a transcript, a log dump, a bullet-list-only markdown file or a CSV-ish export with no terminal punctuation: `sentences` is a single element holding the whole document, the inner loop never fits it, and this branch emits it verbatim. A 200k-token file therefore becomes one 200k-token chunk, which the class docstring calls a "hard token cap per chunk" (line 21) and which downstream blows the extractor's context window on the very first LLM call instead of producing N capped chunks. Nothing logs a warning and nothing caps the emitted size.
- **Code:** `if not buf: # Single sentence exceeds cap — emit as-is buf = [sentences[start]] buf_tokens = token_counts[start] j = start + 1`
- **Fix:** When a single sentence exceeds `max_tokens`, hard-split it on the encoder (`enc.decode(tokens[i:i+max_tokens])`) rather than emitting it whole, and `ctx.log` a warning naming the token count — the same shape `StructuralChunking` uses when it detects an over-cap chunk.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · async · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/ingestion/chunking_strategies/callable_chunking.py:63
**CallableChunking never awaits an async callable that is not a coroutine *function***

- **Impact:** `ChunkFn` is declared as `Callable[[str], list[str]] | Callable[[str], Awaitable[list[str]]]` (line 17), so any awaitable-returning callable is a documented input. But `asyncio.iscoroutinefunction` returns False for a class instance whose `__call__` is `async def`, and for `lambda t: my_async_splitter(t)` — both accepted by the type alias. In those cases `raw_chunks` is a coroutine object, the comprehension on line 77 hits `c.strip()` and raises `AttributeError: 'coroutine' object has no attribute 'strip'` plus a "coroutine was never awaited" RuntimeWarning, instead of chunking. The user sees an attribute error deep in the SDK for a callable the docstring told them to pass.
- **Code:** `if asyncio.iscoroutinefunction(self.fn): raw_chunks = await self.fn(text) else: raw_chunks = self.fn(text) # type: ignore[arg-type]`
- **Fix:** Call the function, then `if inspect.isawaitable(result): result = await result`. That covers coroutine functions, async `__call__` objects and awaitable-returning lambdas in one branch and lets the `# type: ignore` go away.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · async · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/telemetry/tracer.py:90
**Tracer keeps the active span on the instance, so concurrent tasks misattribute and lose spans**

- **Impact:** `_active_span` is plain instance state in an async-first SDK. Two sibling pipeline tasks share one `Tracer` and each open `with tracer.span(...)` around awaits: task A enters (active=A), task A awaits, task B enters and sees `parent = A`, so B is appended to `A.children` and — because `parent is not None` — B is **never** appended to `_spans`. B is not a root span, does not appear in `completed_spans`, and `summary()` prints it indented under an unrelated operation. If B also finishes after A, `self._active_span = parent` restores a span that has already ended. The result is a trace that silently reports the wrong parent/child structure, which is the one thing a tracer exists to get right. `test_tracer.py` only exercises single-threaded nesting, so nothing catches it.
- **Code:** `parent = self._active_span if parent is not None: parent.children.append(s) self._active_span = s try: yield s finally: s.end() self._active_span = parent if parent is None: self._spans.append(s)`
- **Fix:** Hold the active span in a `contextvars.ContextVar[Span | None]`, set/reset it with a token in the `finally`. asyncio propagates context per task, so each task gets its own stack and roots land in `_spans` correctly.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/ingestion/pipeline.py:341
**Zero-chunk documents never get a Document node, breaking update()'s cutover**

- **Impact:** The early return fires *before* Step 3, so `_build_lexical_graph` never runs and no Document node is written. Ingest a file that chunks to nothing (an empty/whitespace-only .txt, or a chunker that filters every candidate): no Document node exists, so the Zero-Loss provenance chain is absent, no `content_hash` is recorded, and every later `ingest()` re-loads and re-chunks it. Worse under `GraphRAG.update()`: the pipeline runs under the transient `<id>__pending__<hex>` DocumentInfo (api/main.py:3572), returns early without creating that pending node, and then `_finish_update` Phase 4 calls `mark_pending_committed(pending_id)`, gets `committed == 0`, and raises `DatabaseError("...may have been concurrently deleted; refusing to proceed")` (api/main.py:3661-3673) — so truncating a tracked document to empty fails the update with a message blaming a concurrent delete. The sibling path does it the house way: `StructuredIngestionPipeline.run` explicitly handles the no-rows case by writing the Document and its hash anyway (structured_pipeline.py:534-541, "Still a state of the table — the one with no rows").
- **Code:** `if not chunks.chunks: ctx.log("No chunks produced, pipeline complete") return IngestionResult(document_info=document.document_info) _assign_deterministic_chunk_uids(document.document_info, chunks)`
- **Fix:** Run step 3 (`_build_lexical_graph`) and `_mark_content_hash` before returning on the empty-chunk path, mirroring structured_pipeline.py:538-541, so an empty document is a real, committable state rather than a missing node.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/ingestion/pipeline.py:364
**Mentions are never filtered against pruned/quality-filtered nodes, so MENTIONED_IN edges silently vanish**

- **Impact:** `_filter_quality` (line 612-616) and `_prune` (line 565-568) both drop relationships whose endpoints no longer exist, but neither touches `graph_data.mentions`. `GraphExtraction` emits an `EntityMention` for every merged entity (graph_extraction.py:835-838). Concrete: ontology declares only `Person`; extraction returns `acme_corp` labelled `Company` with 3 source chunks. `_prune` drops that node (label not in `allowed_labels`, not "Unknown"), but the 3 mentions survive into Step 8, where `upsert_relationships` runs `MATCH (a:__Entity__ {id:'acme_corp'}), (b:Chunk {...}) MERGE ...` (graph_store.py:736-740) — the MATCH finds nothing, zero edges are created, and no error is raised. This is the exact failure mode that `_remap_mentions` was written to fix ("the upsert's MATCH on the merged-away id finds nothing", lines 626-628); the pruning sibling of the same bug class was left unhandled. It also silently inflates the counters (see pipeline-4).
- **Code:** `graph_data = self._filter_quality(graph_data) # Step 5: Prune against ontology ctx.log("Step 5/9: Pruning against ontology") graph_data = self._prune(graph_data, self.ontology)`
- **Fix:** After Step 5, filter mentions to surviving node ids, e.g. `graph_data = graph_data.model_copy(update={"mentions": [m for m in graph_data.mentions if m.entity_id in {n.id for n in graph_data.nodes}]})` — ideally inside `_filter_quality`/`_prune` so all three collections stay consistent in one place.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · async · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/ingestion/pipeline.py:440
**asyncio.gather leaves the sibling step running as an orphan task when one of steps 8/9 raises**

- **Impact:** `asyncio.gather` without `return_exceptions=True` propagates the first exception immediately but does NOT cancel the other task. If `_step_mentions` raises (e.g. `DatabaseError` escaping `upsert_relationships`), `run()` unwinds into the `except Exception` at line 517 and raises `IngestionError`, while `_step_index_chunks` keeps embedding and writing to the vector store unsupervised — after the caller has already handled the failure. Under `GraphRAG.update()` the caller's failure path immediately runs `cleanup_pending_documents(resolved_id)` (api/main.py:3583), deleting the pending Document while the orphan task is still indexing that document's chunks, and the orphan's own eventual exception surfaces only as "Task exception was never retrieved" at loop shutdown. This also breaks the LOAD-BEARING invariant documented at lines 403-428, which assumes both branches have settled before `run()` returns.
- **Code:** `(mentions_written, mentions_shortfall), index_shortfall = await asyncio.gather( _step_mentions(), _step_index_chunks(), )`
- **Fix:** Wrap in `asyncio.TaskGroup` (py3.11) or use `gather(..., return_exceptions=True)` and explicitly cancel/await the surviving task in a `finally` before re-raising, so no write outlives `run()`.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · resource · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/ingestion/backfill.py:242
**Backfill worker tasks leak forever if the chunk iterator raises mid-scan**

- **Impact:** There is no `try/finally` around the producer loop. If the scope iterator raises part-way through (a graph-store connection drop during a paginated chunk scan, or the caller cancelling the outer coroutine), the sentinels are never enqueued and `gather` is never reached: `concurrency` worker tasks stay parked on `await queue.get()` for the lifetime of the event loop, each holding a reference to the LLM client and graph store, and Python emits "Task was destroyed but it is pending" at shutdown. The partially-completed `result` (llm_calls, values_filled) is also lost, so the caller cannot tell how much of the backfill actually landed before the failure.
- **Code:** `workers = [asyncio.create_task(_worker()) for _ in range(self._concurrency)] async for ctx in chunks: await queue.put(ctx) # One sentinel per worker — graceful stop after the queue drains. for _ in workers: await queue.put(None) await asyncio.gather(*workers)`
- **Fix:** Put the producer loop in `try:` and move sentinel-enqueue + `gather` into a `finally:` that cancels the workers on the exception path (`for w in workers: w.cancel()` then `await asyncio.gather(*workers, return_exceptions=True)`).
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/core/providers/_retry.py:40
**is_transient_embedding_error substring-matches "401", so a token-count message misclassifies a retryable batch**

- **Impact:** OpenAI/Azure return the classic oversize-batch error: "Error code: 400 - ... maximum context length is 8192 tokens, however you requested 10401 tokens". The substring "401" is present inside "10401", so `is_transient_embedding_error` returns False, `binary_split_retry_sync` re-raises at line 76, and `LiteLLMEmbedder.embed_documents` fails the whole 2048-text batch — even though splitting the batch is precisely the recovery this module exists to perform. The same false negative fires for any provider message containing a request id, latency, or byte count with "401"/"403" in it.
- **Code:** `msg = str(exc).lower() non_transient = ( "401", "403", "invalid api key", "authentication", "unauthorized", "permission denied", "invalid_api_key", ) return not any(marker in msg for marker in non_transient)`
- **Fix:** Classify on the exception type / HTTP status code the SDKs already expose (`openai.AuthenticationError`, `openai.PermissionDeniedError`, `litellm.AuthenticationError`, or `getattr(exc, "status_code", None) in (401, 403)`) rather than sniffing `str(exc)`. If string matching must stay as a fallback, anchor it (e.g. regex `\b401\b` after stripping digits-with-neighbours) instead of bare `in`.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · resource · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/core/providers/_retry.py:104
**binary_split_retry recursion has no backoff, turning one rate-limited batch into ~4095 immediate requests**

- **Impact:** The provider starts returning HTTP 429 ("rate limit exceeded"). That message contains none of the `non_transient` markers, so every level of the recursion classifies it as transient and splits again with no `asyncio.sleep` anywhere. With the default `batch_size = 2048` (litellm.py:282, openrouter.py:267) a single `aembed_documents` call on one full batch issues 1 + 2 + 4 + ... + 2048 = 4095 requests back-to-back as fast as the event loop allows, all of which also 429. The LLM path in the same package does sleep between attempts (litellm.py:160), so the embedding path is the one that amplifies an outage into a self-inflicted DoS of the caller's own quota.
- **Code:** `mid = len(texts) // 2 left = await binary_split_retry_async(embed_fn, texts[:mid], **kwargs) right = await binary_split_retry_async(embed_fn, texts[mid:], **kwargs) return left + right`
- **Fix:** Add a bounded-depth guard and a jittered `await asyncio.sleep()` before each recursive descent, and treat 429/5xx as "retry the same batch after backoff" rather than "split" — splitting only helps for size/content errors, never for rate limits.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/core/providers/litellm.py:151
**LiteLLM and OpenRouter retry with unjittered 2**attempt, dropping the jitter the base class promises**

- **Impact:** `LLMInterface.ainvoke` (base.py:160) computes `delay = (2**attempt) * (0.5 + random.random())` and its docstring at base.py:138 advertises "retry + jittered exponential backoff". All four provider overrides (litellm.py:151, litellm.py:240, openrouter.py:174, openrouter.py:229) replace it with a constant `2**attempt`. `abatch_invoke` fans out `max_concurrency` (default 12) concurrent `ainvoke` calls; when the provider 429s, all 12 fail at t0, all sleep exactly 1.0s, and all retry at exactly t0+1.0 — a synchronised thundering herd that reproduces the 429 and then repeats at t0+3.0. The jitter that was written specifically to prevent this is present only on the code path the built-in providers never take.
- **Code:** `if attempt < max_retries - 1: delay = 2**attempt logger.warning( "LiteLLM call failed (attempt %d/%d), retrying in %ds: %s",`
- **Fix:** Use one shared backoff helper (see providers-5) that applies the base class's jittered formula, so `LiteLLM`, `OpenRouterLLM` and any future provider inherit the same schedule.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/core/providers/openrouter.py:171
**LLM retry loops retry non-retryable 401/403/400 failures three times with backoff**

- **Impact:** The embedding path has `is_transient_embedding_error` (_retry.py:32) to avoid burning retries on auth failures, but the LLM path has no equivalent: the only exception excluded from retry is `LLMTimeoutError`. With a wrong or missing `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, `ainvoke` issues three 401 round-trips and sleeps 1s + 2s before surfacing the error. Through `abatch_invoke(prompts)` with 1000 prompts that is 3000 requests to an endpoint that has already said the key is invalid, taking 3s per item, and risks IP-level throttling — where one request and an immediate raise was the correct behaviour. A malformed-request 400 (bad `response_format`, unsupported param) behaves identically.
- **Code:** `except LLMTimeoutError: raise except Exception as exc: last_exc = exc if attempt < max_retries - 1: delay = 2**attempt`
- **Fix:** Promote the transient/non-transient classifier out of `_retry.py` into a shared `is_retryable(exc)` used by both the LLM and embedding paths, keyed on SDK exception type / status code, and `raise` immediately for 4xx other than 408/429.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · duplication · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/core/providers/openrouter.py:214
**The 35-line retry loop is copy-pasted four times across litellm.py and openrouter.py, plus a fifth divergent copy in base.py**

- **Impact:** The identical loop body — validate `max_retries`, `validate_timeout`, `last_exc`, `for attempt in range(...)`, `except LLMTimeoutError: raise`, `delay = 2**attempt`, warning + debug log, final error log, `raise last_exc # type: ignore[misc]` — appears at litellm.py:135-171, litellm.py:224-260, openrouter.py:158-194 and openrouter.py:213-249, differing only in the literal string "LiteLLM"/"OpenRouter"/"messages". base.py:146-180 is a fifth copy that additionally applies jitter. That is exactly why providers-3 exists: the jitter fix landed in one copy and not the other four. Any future change (retry budget, retryable-error classification, metrics) must be made in five places, and this repo already has a documented history of a "separate copy of the merge loop" bug from the same pattern.
- **Code:** `last_exc: Exception | None = None for attempt in range(max_retries): try: response = await wait_for_provider_call( client.chat.completions.create(**create_kwargs), timeout=timeout, timeout_error=LLMTimeoutError, operation=f"OpenRouter messages call to {self.model_name}", ) content = response.choices`
- **Fix:** Extract `async def _retry_provider_call(make_awaitable: Callable[[], Awaitable[T]], *, max_retries, timeout, timeout_error, operation) -> T` into `_retry.py` and have base.py, litellm.py and openrouter.py all call it. The only per-site input is the zero-arg factory and the operation label.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · error-handling · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/core/providers/litellm.py:171
**LLMError and EmbeddingError are defined but never raised — raw vendor SDK exceptions escape the abstraction**

- **Impact:** `core/exceptions.py:22` declares `class LLMError(GraphRAGError)` with the docstring "Raised when an LLM provider call fails" and `:34` declares `EmbeddingError` likewise, but a grep over the whole package shows the only place either name appears is their own definitions and the two `*TimeoutError` subclasses. Every non-timeout failure path (`raise last_exc` at litellm.py:171, litellm.py:260, openrouter.py:194, openrouter.py:249, base.py:180, and the re-raise at _retry.py:76) propagates the untouched `openai.APIError` / `litellm.APIConnectionError`. A user who writes `except LLMError:` — the documented contract — catches nothing, and portable code is impossible because handling failures requires importing and branching on the vendor SDK the provider abstraction was supposed to hide. Swapping `LiteLLM` for `OpenRouterLLM` silently changes which exception types a caller must catch.
- **Code:** `raise last_exc # type: ignore[misc]`
- **Fix:** Wrap the terminal raise as `raise LLMError(f"{operation} failed after {max_retries} attempts") from last_exc` (and `EmbeddingError` on the embedding path), preserving `__cause__` so the vendor exception is still reachable. Document the mapping on `LLMInterface`.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · api-contract · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/core/providers/litellm.py:114
**Neither provider reads response.usage or message.tool_calls, so token accounting is absent and tool calls are silently dropped**

- **Impact:** `LLMResponse` (core/models.py:961) declares `tool_calls: list[dict[str, Any]] | None = None`, but all six construction sites in this package (litellm.py:115, litellm.py:145, litellm.py:234, openrouter.py:140, openrouter.py:168, openrouter.py:223) pass `content=` only. A caller doing `llm.invoke(prompt, tools=[...])` — `tools` flows straight through `**kwargs` into `litellm.completion` — gets a response where the model returned no text and only a tool call: `message.content` is `None`, `or ""` turns it into the empty string, `message.tool_calls` is discarded, and the caller receives `LLMResponse(content="", tool_calls=None)` with no error and no way to tell a refusal from a dropped tool call. Separately, `response.usage.prompt_tokens/completion_tokens` is available on every response from both SDKs and is never read, so the SDK cannot report cost or tokens for any ingestion run despite `max_tokens`/`max_completion_tokens` being carefully plumbed.
- **Code:** `response = litellm.completion(**self._completion_kwargs(prompt, **kwargs)) content = response.choices[0].message.content or "" return LLMResponse(content=content)`
- **Fix:** Add a shared `_to_llm_response(response) -> LLMResponse` that populates `content`, `tool_calls` from `choices[0].message.tool_calls`, and a new `usage` field on `LLMResponse` from `response.usage`; call it from all six sites.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/retrieval/strategies/cypher_generation.py:578
**Cypher result parsing turns every short string cell into a fake entity**

- **Impact:** The generated query the prompt itself teaches (template line 200: `RETURN other.name AS name, labels(other) AS type, r.rel_type AS relation, r.fact AS evidence`) returns a rel_type and a fact per row. For a row `("Alice", [...], "FOUNDED", "Alice founded Acme in 1998")`, three cells pass the filter, so `entities` gains `founded` and `alice_founded_acme_in_1998` alongside `alice`. These flow through multi_path.py:275-278 into `found_entities` and out through result_assembly.assemble_raw_result into the `## Key Entities` block, where the answering LLM is told that "Alice founded Acme in 1998" is an entity. They also consume `max_entities` slots ahead of real entities and are then used as `{id: eid}` lookup keys that match nothing.
- **Code:** `for val in row: if ( isinstance(val, str) and len(val) > 1 and len(val) < 100 and not val.startswith(("(", "[", "{")) and not val.replace(".", "").isdigit() ): eid = val.strip().lower().replace(" ", "_") if eid and eid not in entities: entities[eid] = {"name": val.strip(), "description": ""}`
- **Fix:** Only harvest entity names from columns the generator was asked to alias as names (or return node objects and read `id`/`name` off them), rather than pattern-matching arbitrary cell strings.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · resource · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/retrieval/strategies/cypher_generation.py:309
**LIMIT injection is skipped whenever any LIMIT is present, so an LLM-chosen LIMIT 1000000 is executed uncapped**

- **Impact:** The comment claims the sanitiser prevents runaway scans, but it only acts when LIMIT is absent — it never bounds a LIMIT that is present. A model that answers "list every relationship in the graph" with `MATCH (a:__Entity__)-[r:RELATES]-(b:__Entity__) RETURN a.name AS a_name, r.fact AS fact, b.name AS b_name LIMIT 1000000` passes validate_cypher (starts with MATCH, no writes, has RETURN, labels known), is left untouched by _sanitize_cypher, and is executed by graph_store.query_raw. execute_cypher_retrieval then materialises the full result_set into `fact_strings` and an unbounded `entities` dict before anything is truncated (assemble_raw_result only slices `cypher_results[:20]` at the very end). A LIMIT inside a string literal (`WHERE c.text CONTAINS 'limit'`) has the same effect. There is no server-side row cap anywhere on this path.
- **Code:** `# Add LIMIT if missing (prevent runaway scans) if not re.search(r"\bLIMIT\b", cypher, re.IGNORECASE): cypher = cypher.rstrip().rstrip(";") + "\nLIMIT 25"`
- **Fix:** Parse the trailing LIMIT and clamp it to a module constant (e.g. `_MAX_ROWS = 25`), rewriting any larger value, and additionally slice `result.result_set[:_MAX_ROWS]` in execute_cypher_retrieval so a driver that ignores LIMIT cannot blow up memory.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/retrieval/strategies/cypher_generation.py:306
**_sanitize_cypher rewrites the query text after validation and corrupts string literals**

- **Impact:** These substitutions are regex-over-raw-text with no literal awareness, and they run at line 468 *after* `validate_cypher(cypher, ontology)` at line 462, so the string that reaches `graph_store.query_raw` was never validated. For the question "which chunks explain the `path = ...` syntax?" the model emits `MATCH (c:Chunk) WHERE c.text CONTAINS 'path = notation' RETURN c.text AS text LIMIT 25`; the sanitiser silently rewrites the predicate to `CONTAINS 'notation'`, and the user gets results for a different query than the one that was generated and validated. Separately, `MATCH path = (a)-[:RELATES]-(b) RETURN nodes(path) AS ns` becomes `MATCH (a)-[:RELATES]-(b) RETURN nodes(path) AS ns`, which references an undefined variable and fails at execution — the failure is swallowed at line 538-540 and the whole Cypher path returns empty.
- **Code:** `# Remove path variable assignments: "path = MATCH" -> "MATCH" cypher = re.sub(r"\bpath\s*=\s*", "", cypher, flags=re.IGNORECASE)`
- **Fix:** Do the shortestPath/path-variable cleanup as part of generation feedback (reject and retry with the error message, which the retry loop already supports) instead of rewriting, and if any rewriting is kept, re-run `validate_cypher` on the sanitised string before executing it.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · security · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/retrieval/strategies/cypher_generation.py:396
**Label allowlist regex only inspects the first label of a node pattern and misses WHERE-predicate labels**

- **Impact:** The pattern anchors on `(` immediately followed by an optional variable and one colon, so it captures exactly one label per node pattern and nothing outside a node pattern. `MATCH (n:Person:InternalAudit) RETURN n.name AS name LIMIT 25` reports only `Person` and `InternalAudit` is never checked. `MATCH (n) WHERE n:InternalAudit RETURN n.name AS name LIMIT 25` produces zero regex matches, so the loop runs zero times and the query passes label validation entirely. Backtick-quoted labels (`` (n:`Internal Audit`) ``) are likewise unmatched. The docstring promises "the query must start with a read-only keyword, and dangerous constructs are explicitly rejected" with ontology-scoped labels, but the label half of that contract is trivially bypassed, letting generated queries read node classes the ontology deliberately excludes.
- **Code:** `# Check referenced labels exist in ontology allowed_labels = _labels_from_ontology(ontology) | _STRUCTURAL_LABELS label_pattern = re.findall(r"\((?:\w+)?:(\w+)", cypher_norm) for label in label_pattern: if label not in allowed_labels: errors.append(f"Unknown label: {label}")`
- **Fix:** Match every label in a pattern with `re.findall(r":\s*(`[^`]+`|\w+)", cypher_norm)` after stripping string literals, and reject backticked identifiers outright, so multi-label patterns and `WHERE n:Label` predicates are both covered.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · error-handling · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/retrieval/strategies/relationship_expansion.py:52
**1-hop expansion calls .lower() on a possibly-None target name before the None guard**

- **Impact:** `src` is defended with `or ""` but `tgt` is not, and the key tuple is built *before* the truthiness guard. Any `__Entity__` node written without a `name` property (stub nodes created by relationship writes — graph_store.py:609 explicitly carries `is_stub`) makes `b.name` NULL, so `tgt` is None and line 52 raises `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'lower'`. The bare `except Exception` at line 61 swallows it into a debug log, and because the raise happens mid-loop the *entire* 1-hop relationship list is discarded — not just the one bad row — so a single stub node silently removes the whole `## Entity Relationships` section from the LLM context. The 2-hop block 30 lines below does it the right way: it guards all five values at line 84 before building the key at line 85.
- **Code:** `src = row[0] or "" rel_type = row[1] if len(row) > 1 else "" tgt = row[2] if len(row) > 2 else "" fact = row[3] if len(row) > 3 else "" key = (src.lower(), rel_type, tgt.lower()) if src and rel_type and tgt and key not in seen:`
- **Fix:** Apply `or ""` to every extracted cell as the 2-hop block does, and move the key construction below the `if src and rel_type and tgt` guard; also `continue` on a per-row error rather than aborting the loop.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/ingestion/structured_pipeline.py:619
**Node and reference write shortfalls are never checked, so content_hash certifies a partial graph**

- **Impact:** The comment's premise is false. `GraphStore.upsert_nodes` (storage/graph_store.py:196-203) drops nodes whose id does not survive `_clean_identifier`, logs `"Dropped %d %s node(s) with empty id after sanitization"` and returns a SHORT count without raising; `upsert_reference_nodes` (graph_store.py:593-595) does the same with `if not cleaned.strip(): continue`. Both return values are discarded here, so `_reported_short` is applied only to edges. Ingest a table whose key/name column yields an id the sanitizer empties: those Person nodes are silently dropped, `incomplete_writes` stays empty, `_mark_content_hash` records the hash, and the re-ingest of the identical file short-circuits in `update()` as unchanged — the exact no-op-against-missing-data failure the surrounding comment says this design prevents, just moved from edges to nodes.
- **Code:** `# file was a no-op against the missing data. The node writes raise on # failure; the edge writes return a count instead, which is checked. ... await self.graph_store.upsert_nodes(nodes) await self._retract_blank_cells(nodes) ... await self.graph_store.upsert_reference_nodes(_unique_references(refere`
- **Fix:** Capture both return values and apply `_reported_short` to them the way the edge write does: `written = await self.graph_store.upsert_nodes(nodes)` / `if _reported_short(written, len(nodes)): result.incomplete_writes.append(f"nodes {written}/{len(nodes)}")`, and the same for `upsert_reference_nodes` against `len(unique)`. Then correct the comment, which currently states node writes raise.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/ingestion/structured_pipeline.py:540
**Empty-record path discards the lexical shortfall and records content_hash unconditionally**

- **Impact:** `_build_lexical_graph` returns `str | None` — a shortfall description when the store reported fewer lexical edges than handed (lexical_graph.py:143-145). On the normal path (line 614-618) that return value is recorded into `result.incomplete_writes` and gates `_mark_content_hash`. Here it is thrown away. Re-sync a table that has just been emptied (every row's key cell blanked, or the export truncated to a header): if the Document upsert or its PART_OF write is reported short, the hash is still written and `result.incomplete_writes` is empty, so the next `update()` of the same file short-circuits against a Document whose cutover never completed and the previous export's rows stay in the graph forever. The docstring on `_mark_content_hash` says it is "the last step of a run whose every write was reported complete"; this call site violates that.
- **Code:** `ctx.log(f"{source} produced no records; Document written, nothing else") await self._build_lexical_graph(doc_info, chunks, ctx, link_sequential=False) await self._mark_content_hash(doc_info.uid, result.content_hash) return result`
- **Fix:** Mirror the main path: `shortfall = await self._build_lexical_graph(...)`; if it is truthy, append it to `result.incomplete_writes`, log the same warning, and skip `_mark_content_hash`.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/ingestion/structured_pipeline.py:148
**_ambiguous_names counts rows that _walk_records drops, splitting entities the export never mentions twice**

- **Impact:** This loop walks the raw batch and never consults `mapping.anchor`, while `_walk_records` (line 253-258) skips every row whose anchor key cell is blank. Take hr.csv with anchor key `employee_id` and a link `Link("WORKS_AT", to="Organization", by="org_id", name="org_name")`. Row 1: employee_id=E1, org_id=O1, org_name="Acme". Row 5: employee_id blank (row is dropped and never written), org_id=O2, org_name="Acme". `_ambiguous_names` sees `acme__organization` under two distinct keys and marks it ambiguous, so line 812-813 forces BOTH Acme references onto key-derived ids `o1__organization` and `o2__organization`. Acme is split into two placeholder nodes, and a prose mention of "Acme" joins neither — all because of a row that was never loaded. The warning logged at line 158 names a collision that does not exist in the written graph.
- **Code:** `for record in batch: for label, key_column, name_column in named: display = cell_text(record, name_column) key = record.get(key_column) key = "" if key is None else str(key).strip() if display and key: node_id = compute_entity_id(display, label) keys.setdefault(node_id, set()).add(key)`
- **Fix:** Filter the loop through the same skip rule, e.g. iterate `for _i, record, _k, _c in _walk_records(batch, mapping, document_uid)` (passing the uid in) or at minimum `if not cell_text(record, mapping.anchor.key): continue`, so ambiguity is measured over exactly the rows that will be written.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/ingestion/structured_pipeline.py:813
**Key-derived node ids collide under compute_entity_id normalisation with no ambiguity guard**

- **Impact:** `compute_entity_id` (entity_extractors.py:172-177) is `name.strip().lower().replace(" ", "_") + "__" + label.lower()` — it folds case and turns spaces into underscores. `_ambiguous_names` exists precisely because that derivation is not injective, and its docstring spells the case out ("``John Smith`` and ``John_Smith`` are two spellings of one id"), but it only guards the NAME-derived branch. The key-derived fallback on this line has no equivalent check. A products.csv with no name column and keys `SKU 1` and `SKU_1` (or `abc` and `ABC`) derives `sku_1__product` for both rows: two chunks are written, `result.entities` reports 2, `result.records` reports 2, and one node exists in the graph carrying whichever row's properties FalkorDB kept. Nothing warns, and the row that lost is unrecoverable without re-reading the file.
- **Code:** `node_id = compute_entity_id(display, node.label) if display else "" if not node_id or node_id in ambiguous: node_id = compute_entity_id(raw_key, node.label)`
- **Fix:** Extend the collision detection to the key branch: build the same `dict[node_id, set[raw_key]]` over key-derived ids and raise or warn when one id answers to two distinct keys, since unlike a name collision there is no further fallback to demote to.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

So `EdgeMapping(type="WORKS_AT", source="Person", target="Organization", properties={"rel_type": "role_col"})` is accepted, and structured_pipeline.py:881 `properties[edge.signed(prop)] = value` runs AFTER line 871 sets `"rel_type": edge.type`, so every RELATES edge written from this mapping carries the column's value as its rel_type. Every retrieval and cypher-generation path that filters `r.rel_type = 'WORKS_AT'` then finds nothing, and the edges are indistinguishable from correctly typed ones in the graph.

### `high` · resource · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/ingestion/structured_pipeline.py:415
**Every structured ingest materialises the whole file in memory just to emit a warning**

- **Impact:** The comment says "this costs nothing", but `profile_columns` (mapping_proposal.py:111-118) does `seen: dict[str, list[str]] = {column: [] for column in batch.columns}` and appends `cell_text(record, column)` for EVERY row and EVERY column — the entire file held as Python strings at once — and then `_types_holding` casts every filled value against four candidate types, i.e. four more passes over that buffer per column. It runs unconditionally whenever any property is declared STRING (the documented shorthand), and it profiles all columns even though only `declared_strings` are consulted. On a 1M-row x 20-column export this allocates ~20M str objects (multiple GB) and burns ~80M `Column.cast` calls, purely to decide whether to log a `logger.warning`. `CsvRecordLoader` is explicitly documented as holding "nothing in memory beyond the current row"; this call undoes that guarantee.
- **Code:** `# silence. The file is already walked twice here, so this costs nothing. profiles = profile_columns(batch, sample_rows=batch.record_count or _WHOLE_FILE)`
- **Fix:** Profile only the columns in `declared_strings`, stream the type-narrowing check without buffering values (fold each cell into a per-column surviving-type set as it is read), and cap it at a bounded sample with the whole-file guarantee applied only to the narrow set of declared-STRING columns.

### `high` · resource · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/core/providers/base.py:74
**Provider timeouts cancel the awaiting task but leak the asyncio.to_thread worker thread**

- **Impact:** A user runs completion() under ctx.latency_budget_ms, so ctx.provider_timeout_seconds() supplies a timeout to LLMInterface.ainvoke (base.py:149) and Embedder.aembed_documents (base.py:94). Those wrap a blocking provider SDK call in asyncio.to_thread; asyncio.wait_for cancels the awaiting Task on timeout, but a thread already running inside the default ThreadPoolExecutor cannot be cancelled and keeps blocking on its socket until the provider's own (often absent) timeout fires. The default executor has min(32, cpu_count+4) workers — on a 4-core box, 8. After 8 timed-out LLM/embedding calls every remaining asyncio.to_thread in the process — TextLoader.load, PdfLoader.load, GLiNERExtractor.extract_entities, SpacyExtractor — queues behind dead threads and the whole pipeline hangs with no error.
- **Code:** `return await wait_for_provider_call( asyncio.to_thread(self.embed_query, text, **kwargs), timeout=timeout, timeout_error=EmbeddingTimeoutError, # core/providers/_timeout.py:19 return await asyncio.wait_for(awaitable, timeout=timeout)`
- **Fix:** Do not put a cancellable timeout around an uncancellable to_thread. Either push the deadline into the provider SDK (pass its own request timeout) so the thread itself gives up, or run these calls on a dedicated bounded ThreadPoolExecutor via loop.run_in_executor so a leaked worker cannot starve the shared default pool.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · async · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/discovery/pipeline.py:537
**discover_ontology gathers every source at once, unbounded and without return_exceptions**

- **Impact:** Ontology.from_sources(glob("corpus/**/*.pdf")) with 2,000 files: gather launches 2,000 _process_document coroutines at once. The chunk_sem only guards the LLM calls (pipeline.py:428, 440) — the load and chunk steps at pipeline.py:412-413 are ungated, so 2,000 PdfLoader.load calls hit asyncio.to_thread simultaneously (exhausting the default executor) and every document's full text plus chunk list is held in memory at the same time. Worse, there is no return_exceptions=True: one unreadable PDF raising LoaderError aborts the whole gather and cancels the other 1,999 documents mid-flight, throwing away every LLM call already paid for. The same shape repeats at discovery/pipeline.py:828 (per_source = await asyncio.gather(*[_process(s) for s in sources_list])). BackfillExecutor.run (ingestion/backfill.py:200-206) documents the correct pattern for exactly this — a bounded queue plus N worker tasks, 'without this pattern, a 50k-chunk backfill would create 50k pending tasks up front'.
- **Code:** `per_doc = await asyncio.gather( *[ _process_document( llm, src, loader=loader, chunker=used_chunker,`
- **Fix:** Wrap _process_document in the same chunk_sem (or a separate document-level semaphore) so loading and chunking are bounded too, and pass return_exceptions=True, logging and skipping the failed sources so one bad file does not discard the corpus.

### `high` · error-handling · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/discovery/pipeline.py:449

One rate-limited chunk call therefore destroys a multi-hour, multi-document discovery run — while _run_chunk_proposal's docstring promises 'Returns None on exhausted retries so the doc-level merge can simply skip this chunk — soft-fail at the pipeline level' and _run_doc_summary's promises 'the pipeline keeps going'.

- **Fix:** Broaden the handler in _run_chunk_proposal and _run_doc_summary to `except Exception` (re-raising LatencyBudgetExceededError and CancelledError), or have extract_with_retry wrap any provider exception in OntologyDiscoveryError so the documented soft-fail actually holds.

**CsvRecordLoader.load_records parses the entire CSV synchronously inside an async def**

Under apply_changes(added=[...], max_concurrency=3) or any concurrent retrieve(), that is multiple seconds during which no other coroutine runs, no LLM response is read and no DB reply is processed. Every sibling loader in the same package does the opposite: TextLoader.load (text_loader.py:28), PdfLoader.load (pdf_loader.py:32) and MarkdownLoader.load (markdown_loader.py:30) are one-liners that delegate with `return await asyncio.to_thread(self._load_sync, source)`.

- **Fix:** Split the body into a sync _load_records_sync(source) and make load_records `return await asyncio.to_thread(self._load_records_sync, source)`, matching TextLoader/PdfLoader/MarkdownLoader.

### `high` · async · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/ingestion/chunking_strategies/sentence_token_cap.py:82
**Chunkers run tiktoken over the whole document on the event loop, including a blocking first-call download**

- **Impact:** SentenceTokenCapChunking is the default chunker for every ingest (api/main.py:_ingest_single). chunk() is declared async but contains no await: on the first call in a fresh container tiktoken.get_encoding() performs a blocking HTTPS download of the cl100k_base BPE file, and on every call it then BPE-encodes each sentence of the whole document. For a 2MB PDF that is several seconds of CPU with the event loop pinned — during an _ingest_batch(max_concurrency=3) the other two documents' in-flight LLM responses cannot be read and their provider timeouts can fire spuriously. StructuralChunking.chunk_document (structural_chunking.py:90) and ContextualChunking._enrich_chunks (contextual_chunking.py:149, which also enc.encode()s the entire document text) have the same shape. The loaders one directory over show the house pattern: `return await asyncio.to_thread(self._load_sync, source)`.
- **Code:** `async def chunk(self, text: str, ctx: Context) -> TextChunks: ... enc = tiktoken.get_encoding(self.encoding_name) sentences = [s.strip() for s in _SENTENCE_END.split(text.strip()) if s.strip()] token_counts = [len(enc.encode(s)) for s in sentences]`
- **Fix:** Move the tokenizing body of each chunker into a sync helper and have the async `chunk` / `chunk_document` return `await asyncio.to_thread(...)`, the way the loaders do.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · resource · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/storage/deduplicator.py:925
**Fuzzy dedup's "block-wise to avoid OOM" similarity loop is still O(n) memory per block**

- **Impact:** finalize(fuzzy=True) on a 400k-entity graph: _fetch_all_entities (line 1347) materialises all 400k rows, aembed_documents embeds all 400k names in one call, and then the very first iteration computes block(1000 x d) @ remaining.T(d x 400000), allocating a 1000 x 400000 float32 array = 1.6 GB for a single intermediate — the comment on line 921 claims this loop exists 'to avoid OOM' but only the left operand is blocked. The process OOMs at exactly the step the comment says is safe. _fetch_all_entities is additionally called four times in one deduplicate() run (_deduplicate_exact:624, _deduplicate_fuzzy:893, _deduplicate_with_resolver:991, _report_near_misses:588), re-reading the entire entity set from FalkorDB each time.
- **Code:** `# Find pairs above threshold (block-wise to avoid OOM) BLOCK_SIZE = 1000 ... for i_start in range(0, n, BLOCK_SIZE): block = mat_normed[i_start : min(i_start + BLOCK_SIZE, n)] remaining = mat_normed[i_start:] sim_block = block @ remaining.T`
- **Fix:** Block both operands (nested loop over j_start, computing block_i @ block_j.T and keeping only j_start+lc > i_start+lr), or use a chunked top-k search. Fetch the entity set once per deduplicate() call and pass it down instead of re-querying in each phase.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · resource · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/storage/vector_store.py:331
**embed_relationships writes one query per edge and can loop 10,000 times making no progress**

- **Impact:** Two problems in one loop. (a) finalize() on a graph with 50k RELATES edges issues 50k sequential round-trips, one per edge, where the sibling backfill_entity_embeddings (line 740) writes the same shape with a single UNWIND batch and only falls back to per-item on batch failure — same for index_chunks (line 245). (b) The outer paginator re-runs `WHERE r.embedding IS NULL LIMIT 500` with no SKIP, relying on writes removing rows from the filter. If the writes fail (an index-write permission error, a vector-dimension rejection), total_embedded stays 0, the same 500 edges come back, and the guard above only covers empty vectors — so the loop runs the full _MAX_PAGINATION_ITERATIONS = 10,000 times, issuing ~5 million failing queries before the for/else logs 'Pagination exceeded'.
- **Code:** `for item in embed_data: try: await self._conn.query( "MATCH ()-[r:RELATES]->() " "WHERE id(r) = $rid " "SET r.embedding = vecf32($vector)", item, ) total_embedded += 1 except Exception as inner_exc: logger.warning(f"Failed to embed edge {item['rid']}: {inner_exc}")`
- **Fix:** Write the batch with a single UNWIND (mirroring backfill_entity_embeddings) with a per-item fallback, and break out of the paginator when an iteration embeds zero edges, exactly as the empty-vector guard already does.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · api-contract · docs/api-reference.mdx:149
**api-reference says completion() ignores prompt_template with history and uses ainvoke() without it; both false**

- **Impact:** `completion()` has one unified path: `template = prompt_template or _RAG_PROMPT` then `final_user_content = template.format(...)` (graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/api/main.py:4329-4330), and it always calls `await self.llm.ainvoke_messages(messages, ...)` (api/main.py:4338) whether or not history was passed. The method's own docstring (api/main.py:4258-4260) says prompt_template is "Applied in both single-turn and multi-turn modes", directly contradicting line 149. Consequences: (a) a caller who adds history expecting their custom template to drop out silently keeps it, including its escaping rules -- and `_neutralize_context_close_tag` is skipped for custom templates (api/main.py:4309-4317), so this is a prompt-injection-relevant misunderstanding; (b) a custom provider that overrode `ainvoke()` for native async but not `ainvoke_messages()` goes through the base concatenating default on every single-turn completion, not just multi-turn ones. docs/providers.mdx:150 and docs/api-reference.mdx:328 repeat (b).
- **Code:** `| `prompt_template` | `str \| None` | `None` | Custom prompt for single-turn (must contain `{context}` and `{question}`). Ignored when `history` is provided. | ... When history is provided, `completion()` builds a native messages list: `[system_prompt, *history, user_question]` and calls `LLMInterfa`
- **Fix:** State that `prompt_template` applies in both modes and that `ainvoke_messages()` is the sole generation entry point, so providers should override it rather than `ainvoke()` if they want native chat.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

**Third copy of the merged_labels fold in exact_match_merge drops a duplicate's previously-absorbed labels**

This copy never reads `s.properties['merged_labels']` -- it only collects `s.label`. Concretely: document 1 resolves an entity to a survivor labelled `Author` carrying `merged_labels='Illustrator'`. Line 506-512 then appends only `'Author'`. `'Illustrator'` is gone, so `GraphStore._promote_merged_labels` never stamps it and the node is invisible to `MATCH (n:Illustrator)` -- exactly the defect the sibling comment says this class of code exists to prevent.
