### `high` · duplication · graphrag_sdk/src/graphrag_sdk/ingestion/resolution_strategies/llm_verified_resolution.py:1058
**Union-Find implemented three times, and the two copies in one file disagree on return type and gate condition**

- **Impact:** The identical path-halving `find` body appears three times: `ingestion/resolution_strategies/llm_verified_resolution.py:1058` and `:1525`, and `storage/deduplicator.py:846`. `storage/deduplicator.py:59` already carries this exact bug class in its own comment history — `ingestion/resolution_strategies/exact_match.py:50-58` documents three past defects whose "cause each time was a separate copy of the merge loop" — and the same shape has recurred here. The two copies inside `llm_verified_resolution.py` have already drifted: `union` at line 1064 returns `bool` and gates on `if self.label_family_gate:` (line 1069), while `union` at line 1531 returns `None` and gates on `if self.unified_stage and self.label_family_gate:` (line 1537). Concretely: construct `LLMVerifiedResolution(label_family_gate=True, unified_stage=False)` and feed it a Person~Engineer / Engineer~Institution chain — the PASS-2 copy at line 1064 blocks the transitive union and the unified-stage copy at line 1531 permits it, so the same object merges or refuses to merge the same two entities depending only on which code path the caller entered. The 40-line comments at lines 1046-1051 and 1503-1508 both explain the gate, in different words, for the same rule.
- **Code:** `def find(x: int) -> int: while parent[x] != x: parent[x] = parent[parent[x]] x = parent[x] return x def union(x: int, y: int) -> bool: nonlocal blocked_unions rx, ry = find(x), find(y) if rx == ry: return False if self.label_family_gate:`
- **Fix:** Extract one `_FamilyGatedUnionFind` helper (parent list/dict, `cluster_families`, `blocked_unions`, `find`, `union`) taking the gate predicate as a constructor argument, into `ingestion/resolution_strategies/base.py` beside the other shared merge rules (`description_list`, `set_merged_descriptions`, `merge_source_ids`), and call it from all three sites. Settle the `bool` vs `None` return and the `unified_stage` term deliberately while doing so — the divergence is currently undocumented.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/tests/test_cypher_generation.py:138
**_sanitize_cypher's path-assignment rewrite runs after validation and is only tested for the benign case**

- **Impact:** `_sanitize_cypher` (cypher_generation.py:306) applies `re.sub(r"\\bpath\\s*=\\s*", "", cypher, flags=re.IGNORECASE)` unconditionally and, per generate_cypher lines 460-466, runs AFTER `validate_cypher` has approved the query — so nothing re-checks what it produces. Any ontology declaring a property called `path` (or the structural `Document.path` the SDK itself writes, see graph_store.set_document_path) makes the LLM emit `MATCH (d:Document) WHERE d.path = 'report.pdf' RETURN d.id`. Validation passes, then the sanitizer rewrites it to `MATCH (d:Document) WHERE d.'report.pdf' RETURN d.id`, FalkorDB raises a syntax error, `execute_cypher_retrieval` swallows it at line 536 and returns `([], {})` — the user gets a silent "no context found" answer for a perfectly valid question, with no error surfaced anywhere. The single sanitize test only covers `MATCH path = (...)`, so this corruption is invisible.
- **Code:** `def test_removes_path_assignment(self): cypher = "MATCH path = (a)-[:RELATES]->(b) RETURN a, b" result = _sanitize_cypher(cypher) assert "path =" not in result and "path=" not in result`
- **Fix:** Add cases to TestSanitizeCypher asserting a property access named `path` survives untouched: `_sanitize_cypher("MATCH (d:Document) WHERE d.path = 'x' RETURN d.id")` must still contain `d.path = 'x'`. Also add a test that whatever `_sanitize_cypher` returns still passes `validate_cypher`, which pins the ordering invariant that generate_cypher relies on.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/tests/test_facade.py:214
**test_ingest_auto_detects_pdf asserts nothing about loader selection — only that some exception is raised**

- **Impact:** `/fake/file.pdf` does not exist, so TextLoader, MarkdownLoader, PdfLoader or no loader at all would each raise. The test would pass unchanged if the `.pdf` branch in `GraphRAG.ingest` were deleted and every source fell through to TextLoader — which is the exact regression its name and docstring claim to catch. It is also the only `pytest.raises(Exception)` in the whole 36k-line suite, so it is inconsistent with the sibling immediately below it, `test_ingest_auto_detects_md` (line 224), which spies on `IngestionPipeline.__init__` and asserts `isinstance(captured_loader, MarkdownLoader)`.
- **Code:** `async def test_ingest_auto_detects_pdf(self, mock_conn, embedder, llm): """Verifies PDF extension triggers PdfLoader selection.""" g = GraphRAG(connection=mock_conn, llm=llm, embedder=embedder, embedding_dimension=8) # We won't actually load a PDF, just verify the loader type # The loader selection `
- **Fix:** Rewrite it in the shape of `test_ingest_auto_detects_md`: monkeypatch `IngestionPipeline.__init__` to capture the `loader` argument, stub `run`/`ensure_indices`/`_write_graph_config`, then `assert isinstance(captured_loader, PdfLoader)`. Better still, extract the loader-selection logic into a small pure helper and table-test `.pdf`/`.md`/`.txt`/no-extension against it.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · dead-code · graphrag_sdk/tests/test_entity_extractors.py:158
**gliner ImportError test can never run: gliner is a hard install dependency, so the test always hits pytest.skip**

- **Impact:** `gliner>=0.2,<1` is in `[project].dependencies` in graphrag_sdk/pyproject.toml — not an optional extra — and CI installs with `pip install -e ".[dev]"` (ci.yml:43, ci.yml:75). So `import gliner` always succeeds and this test unconditionally skips in every environment the project supports. `GLiNERExtractor` is also the default extractor, and the `except ImportError: raise ImportError("GLiNER is required...")` branch at entity_extractors.py:673-676 has zero coverage: if someone changes that message, drops the `match="GLiNER"` contract, or moves the import so the error surfaces as a bare ModuleNotFoundError, nothing in the suite notices. The file reads as if the branch is covered — a maintainer grepping for `test_import_error` sees a green test. Contrast the sibling `TestFastCorefResolver.test_import_error_message` (test_coref_resolvers.py:31), which guards the same way but genuinely runs, because `fastcoref` really is an optional extra.
- **Code:** `class TestGLiNERExtractor: async def test_import_error_when_gliner_missing(self): try: import gliner # noqa: F401 pytest.skip("gliner is installed") except ImportError: extractor = GLiNERExtractor() with pytest.raises(ImportError, match="GLiNER"): await extractor.extract_entities("text", ["Person"],`
- **Fix:** Drop the try/import/skip dance and force the failure deterministically: `monkeypatch.setitem(sys.modules, "gliner", None)` (or patch `builtins.__import__`) so the ImportError branch is exercised on every run, then assert the message. If the branch is genuinely unreachable now that gliner is a hard dependency, delete both the test and the dead try/except in entity_extractors.py rather than leaving a permanently-skipped test.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/tests/test_backfill_integration.py:16
**test_backfill_integration.py never runs anywhere — CI sets RUN_INTEGRATION but not OPENAI_API_KEY**

- **Impact:** The integration job in .github/workflows/ci.yml sets exactly three env vars (lines 66-69): `RUN_INTEGRATION`, `FALKOR_HOST`, `FALKOR_PORT`. `OPENAI_API_KEY` is set in neither job, so this module's `skipif` is always true and every test in it is skipped in both CI jobs and in any developer run that does not manually export a key. The module's own docstring asserts the invariant it claims to guard — "add_attribute is atomic: declare + LLM backfill + commit" — so `add_attribute`'s atomicity has no executing test at all. Worse, the CI step comment at ci.yml:92-95 states "every test gated on RUN_INTEGRATION (the structured-ingestion, **backfill**, cached-extraction and ontology-evolution suites) runs here against the live service", which is false for this file; a maintainer reading CI believes backfill is covered. Compare test_ontology_evolve_integration.py:18, whose skipif gates on `RUN_INTEGRATION` alone and therefore really does run.
- **Code:** `pytestmark = pytest.mark.skipif( os.getenv("RUN_INTEGRATION") != "1" or not os.getenv("OPENAI_API_KEY"), reason="Requires RUN_INTEGRATION=1 and OPENAI_API_KEY", )`
- **Fix:** Either add `OPENAI_API_KEY: ${{ secrets.OPENAI_API_KEY }}` to the integration job's `env` block and a guard that fails the job when the secret is absent on main, or rewrite these tests against a deterministic scripted LLM (the pattern `conftest._scripted_extraction_llm` already provides) so they run with FalkorDB alone. Until one of those lands, correct the misleading comment at ci.yml:92-95 so it no longer claims the backfill suite is exercised.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

### `high` · correctness · graphrag_sdk/tests/test_structured_pipeline.py:66
**RecordingGraphStore.__getattr__ returns an AsyncMock for any name, so a renamed or misspelled store method silently no-ops**

- **Impact:** This catch-all makes every attribute access on the fake store succeed and return an awaitable yielding 0. `StructuredIngestionPipeline` calls a wide surface of GraphStore — `_retract_blank_cells` (structured_pipeline.py:651), `release_entity_keys`, `drop_node_property`, `rows_outside_document`, `strip_stale_provenance`. If any of those is renamed in graph_store.py, or a call site develops a typo, the 21 tests in TestStructuredIngest still pass: the call resolves to a fresh AsyncMock, returns 0, and `store.nodes` / `store.relationships` still contain what the write path put there. Concretely: rename `GraphStore.drop_node_property` to `remove_node_property` in the source and leave the pipeline calling the old name — production raises AttributeError on every structured ingest, and this file reports green. Only the integration suite (which needs FalkorDB and RUN_INTEGRATION) would catch it. The explicitly-defined stubs above it (`upsert_nodes`, `resolve_by_entity_key`, `reconcile_keyed_identity`, lines 45-65) show the author knew which methods matter; the fallback undoes that discipline for everything else.
- **Code:** `def __getattr__(self, name): # The lexical writer touches more of the store than this test needs. return AsyncMock(return_value=0)`
- **Fix:** Build the fake from the real contract instead of a wildcard: use `MagicMock(spec=GraphStore)` with explicit `AsyncMock` side-effects for the methods under test (the pattern conftest.mock_graph_store already uses at conftest.py:256), or keep the hand-written class but replace `__getattr__` with an explicit allowlist that raises `AssertionError(f"unexpected graph_store call: {name}")` for anything not in it. Either way an unknown call becomes a loud failure rather than a silent zero.
- **Verification:** 0/3 lenses voted, 0 refuted

## Style summaries per area

**core-models** — The five files mostly follow the house contract: all carry the "# GraphRAG SDK — Core: <Thing>" header, all have `from __future__ import annotations`, and models.py, context.py and connection.py declare the module-level logger (circuit_breaker.py is the one exception, and it logs no state transitions as a result). Docstring quality is unusually high in places — connection.query, Ontology.from_sources, FinalizeResult's per-field docs and GraphData all use full Google-style sections with Sphinx roles and Example:: blocks — but the coverage is uneven within single files: BatchEntry's three public members have no docstrings at all next to GraphData.extraction_failed's seven-line one, and reject_reserved_labels/stable_document_id/ensure_no_pending_marker have long prose with no Args:/Returns:/Raises: sections while their sibling from_sources has all three. Typing is the weakest area against the stated "Any is discouraged" rule: Ontology.from_sources annotates six parameters as `Any | None` that discovery/pipeline.py types precisely (Context, Catalog, LLMInterface), Context.child takes untyped **overrides, and RawSearchResult.records is list[Any] — all latent mypy --strict debt in files that are otherwise fully annotated. Pydantic usage is v2 except the base DataModel, which still uses the deprecated `class Config` while discovery/proposal.py and discovery/pipeline.py use `model_config = ConfigDict(...)`. The most consequential doc problems are not formatting but accuracy: three comments/docstrings assert behaviour the code does not have (a reserved-attribute Ontology validator, a Context.tracer field, a construction-time server probe), and the public docs page contradicts the real signatures of ctx.log, Context's budget default, from_url's kwargs and the retry-delay formula.

**deduplicator** — The module follows the house contract closely on the mechanical points: the `# GraphRAG SDK — Storage: Entity Deduplicator` header, `from __future__ import annotations`, `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)`, module constants with `#:` comments, async-first methods, and ruff-clean 100-column formatting. Type hints are present on all 36 signatures, but two diverge from the house way — `graph_store: Any` on `__init__` (the real `GraphStore` exists and is what api/main.py passes) and `list[list[dict]]` on `_merge_acronym_groups`, both of which `mypy --strict` (configured in pyproject, not run in CI) would reject. Docstring quality is unusually rich in prose but inconsistent in structure: `_deduplicate_with_resolver`, `_survivor_rank` and `_adopt_into_declared_labels` carry long, evidence-backed explanations with Sphinx roles, while their siblings `_deduplicate_exact` and `_deduplicate_fuzzy` have none at all, and the module's only public method, `deduplicate()`, has no `Args:`/`Returns:` sections although the private `_remap_entity_edges` does and the public wrapper in api/main.py documents the same parameters in full. Logging mixes f-strings (5 sites: lines 552, 681, 707, 767, 951, 1400) with lazy %-style (~15 sites), which matches the repo's own split habit rather than a single convention. The one substantive style-as-correctness divergence is duplication: `_merge_description_segments`/`union_chunk_ids` here re-implement `set_merged_descriptions`/`merge_source_ids` from ingestion/resolution_strategies/base.py with different semantics, which is the finding in deduplicator-3.

**discovery** — The package is above the repo average on the mechanical parts of the house contract and below it on typing and docstring uniformity. All six modules carry the `# GraphRAG SDK — <Area>: <Thing>` header and `from __future__ import annotations`; the three modules that log (pipeline.py, catalog.py, instructor.py) all declare `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)` module-level, and proposal.py/prompts.py correctly omit it. proposal.py and instructor.py are the exemplars — full Google sections with `Args:`/`Returns:`/`Raises:`, Sphinx roles (`:py:meth:`graphrag_sdk.GraphRAG.suggest_schema_extensions``) and an `Example::` block in `DBpediaCatalog` — and the "why" comments throughout pipeline.py (the semaphore rationale at 424-427, the `model_copy` rationale at 264-266) are genuinely good. Typing is where it slips: nine signatures across prompts.py and catalog.py are either unannotated or blanket-suppressed with `# type: ignore[no-untyped-def]` (prompts.py:54, 110, 112, 145; catalog.py:88 twice, 493, 501), all of which mypy --strict would reject and none of which CI catches. Docstring coverage is inconsistent between direct siblings rather than between files: five helpers (`_attr_from_proposed`, `_entity_from_proposed`, `_relation_from_proposed`, `_strip_fences`, `_render_schema_block`) have none while the function immediately next to each has a full one. Two comments actively contradict the code beneath them (pipeline.py:718-721, pipeline.py:909-912) and one module header miscounts its own API (catalog.py:4), which is the most maintainer-hostile category here because the prose reads as authoritative.

**exports** — The export surface is mostly well kept — the root `__all__` is exactly complete and accurate (every one of the ~100 imported names is listed and every listed name is imported, verified by diff), the deprecation shims are real and tested, and optional dependencies are correctly imported lazily inside functions (litellm, openai, gliner, spacy, fastcoref, pypdf all stay out of module scope), so `import graphrag_sdk` does not require any extra. Where it diverges from the house contract is in the package inits themselves: 15 of 16 lack `from __future__ import annotations` (only discovery/__init__.py has it, against ~100% adoption in implementation modules), all 16 use `#` comments where a module docstring belongs so the package tree is invisible to `help()` and autodoc, and 4 `__getattr__` shims omit the `-> Any` return type that the one correct copy (core/models.py:1255) has and that `strict = true` in pyproject requires. The bigger consistency problem is between levels: which of the root, the subpackage or the leaf package exports a given built-in follows no rule — StructuralChunking, LocalRetrieval, MarkdownLoader, PdfLoader, LLMResponse and ExtractedEntity are each documented as public yet reachable only via a deep module path, while their direct siblings are top-level. Finally the docs have drifted furthest of all: eight pages including both READMEs and the getting-started quickstart still teach the four deprecated v1.2 aliases and the deprecated `schema=` kwarg, and docs/api-reference.mdx documents `EntityType`/`GraphSchema` as if they were classes when they are `__getattr__` aliases.

**extraction** — These six files are well above the repo average on documentation intent but uneven on execution. All five non-`__init__` modules carry the `# GraphRAG SDK — <Area>: <Thing>` header, `from __future__ import annotations` and a module-level `logger`; `__init__.py` is the only one missing the future import. Type hints are essentially complete, with `Any` confined to places where third-party model objects genuinely have no type (`gliner`, `spacy`, `fastcoref`). The prose standard set by `entity_extractors.py` is unusually high — measured benchmark numbers, explicit "do not remove this again" notes, `Raises:` sections — which makes the gaps stand out as oversights: `GraphExtraction.extract`, `_entities_to_nodes`, `_relations_to_relationships` and `_format_property_for_prompt` have no docstring at all (extraction-15), `FastCorefResolver.resolve` has none while its ABC does (extraction-19), and `CachedChunkExtraction.extract` has a two-line summary with no Args/Returns against the ABC's full Google block. Docstring format is otherwise consistent (summary line, blank line, prose, `Args:`/`Returns:`, Sphinx roles and `Example::` in `base.py` and `CompositeExtractor`), with one outright contradiction where `CachedChunkExtraction._merge` claims an equivalence with `_aggregate_relations` that the code does not have (extraction-2). Line length stays inside the 100-char ruff limit throughout — the apparent violations are box-drawing section banners counted in bytes rather than characters. The clearest consistency failure is not cosmetic: three near-identical merge/dedup loops (`_aggregate_relations`, `_graph_data_from_cache`'s `rel_by_pair`, `CachedChunkExtraction._merge`) and two near-identical prediction parsers (`_parse_predictions`, `LLMExtractor._parse_response`) have each drifted from their siblings in ways that change behaviour (extraction-1, -2, -3, -13), and `coref_resolvers.py` omits the shared-model cache and load lock that both sibling extractors implement and document at length (extraction-7).

**facade-core** — api/main.py is unusually well documented for a 5101-line facade: essentially every public method carries a Google-style docstring with Args/Returns/Raises, Sphinx roles (:py:meth:, :py:class:) and Example:: blocks, and the file's comment culture is genuinely load-bearing — several comments record the measured failure a line exists to prevent. The module header, `from __future__ import annotations`, and `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)` all follow the contract. The main divergences are concentrated and mechanical: four bare `-> Any` return annotations where the concrete type exists (three async generators at 1321/1467/1611 and _owner_for_kind at 721), one unannotated class attribute (_TABULAR_SUFFIXES at 2554), two private helpers with no docstring at all (_table_signed at 1287, _is_tabular at 2557) among roughly thirty siblings that have full ones, five function-local imports (308, 453, 466, 1503, 4363) that are not breaking any cycle, and one docstring (update()) that documents two parameters twice and under-declares its Raises. Encapsulation is the weakest axis: the facade writes collaborators' private attributes at five sites (_graph_store._BATCH_SIZE at 1192/1245, _vector_store._indices_ensured at 1767/1768/4707) and imports two underscore-prefixed names across module boundaries (_strip_markdown_fences, _coerce_attribute_value at 66-73). Because mypy is configured strict in pyproject but not run in CI, the typing gaps above are real latent debt rather than caught noise.

**facade-quality** — The module follows most of the house contract faithfully: the `# GraphRAG SDK — API: GraphRAG Facade` header with `# Pattern:` / `# Principle:` lines is present, `from __future__ import annotations` is at the top, `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)` is module-level, and the public surface is async-first with explicit thin `*_sync` wrappers that carry "Keep in sync with :meth:`x`" notes. Docstring coverage is unusually high for a 5101-line file — of roughly 75 methods, only `_table_signed` (1287), `_is_tabular` (2557), the `_global_ontology` getter (392), `__aenter__`/`__aexit__` (422/425) and the twelve nested `_iter`/`_prompt`/`_parse`/`_merge` closures lack one — and the better docstrings (`query`, `ingest`, `update`, `_same_embedding_model`) use Google-style Args/Returns/Raises, Sphinx `:py:class:`/`:meth:` roles and `Example::` blocks exactly as the contract describes. Typing is otherwise complete, so the `Any` returns stand out: four sites (`_owner_for_kind` at 721 and three `async def _iter() -> Any` at 1321/1467/1611) use `Any` where a real type exists, and six `__init__` attributes (`_config_validated`, `_docs_since_dedup`, `_finalize_reminder_emitted`, `_enable_cypher`, `_cypher_hint_logged`, `_embedding_dimension`) and the `_TABULAR_SUFFIXES` class attribute carry no annotation while `_mapping_changes: set[str]` and `_natural_mappings: dict[str, TableMapping]` right beside them do. The consistency gaps that matter most are behavioural rather than cosmetic: `update()` and `ingest()` diverge on which kwargs a tabular source rejects, the same helper logic is copied 2-4 times in four separate places (rebind guard, pending-cleanup handler, backfill count reconciliation, ontology re-propagation), and roughly 450 lines of table-mapping reconciliation sit in the facade rather than in `core/tables.py` or `ingestion/`.

**graph-store** — The module gets the structural house rules right: it has the "# GraphRAG SDK — Storage: Graph Store" header with Pattern/Origin lines, `from __future__ import annotations`, and a module-level `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)` (though at line 49, below the ReferenceNode class rather than at the top as siblings place it), and nearly every identifier interpolation routes through `sanitize_cypher_label` — the one exception at line 134 is finding graph-store-1. Docstring coverage is high in volume but very inconsistent in form: across 57 async methods there are only 5 `Args:` sections, 5 `Returns:` and 1 `Raises:`, so the Google-style contract is honoured by a handful of methods (upsert_nodes, upsert_relationships, rollforward_cutover, release_entity_keys) while the majority — reconcile_keyed_identity, delete_stale_relationships, resolve_by_entity_key, query_raw — use long free prose with no sections, and several document behaviour the code does not implement (graph-store-5, graph-store-6, graph-store-14). Sphinx roles (`:meth:`, `:func:`, `:class:`) appear in about six docstrings and are absent from their immediate neighbours. Logging is split roughly two-to-one between lazy %-style and eager f-strings, sometimes forty lines apart inside one method (graph-store-17), and four class attributes in the same block as an annotated one carry no annotations (graph-store-16), which is exactly the kind of latent debt that goes uncaught because mypy is configured strict but not run in CI.

**ontology-store** — core/tables.py follows the house contract closely: the `# GraphRAG SDK — Core: table declaration value types` header, `from __future__ import annotations`, Google-style docstrings with `Args:` / `Raises:` sections and `Example::` blocks on `Column`, `Link` and `TableMapping`, and full annotations on every signature. Its two divergences are structural rather than cosmetic — it uses stdlib dataclasses rather than Pydantic v2 (defensible for value types embedded in a Pydantic `Ontology`, but undocumented), and it mixes `frozen=True` on `Column` with mutable `Link` / `TableMapping`, so the `_check_label` and `_check_identifier` guards that keep names safe for Cypher can be bypassed by post-construction assignment. storage/ontology_store.py is the outlier in its package: it is the only one of the five modules in `storage/` that opens with a module docstring instead of the `# GraphRAG SDK — Storage: <Thing>` header comment, and across 33 methods it contains zero `Args:` / `Returns:` / `Raises:` sections against 12 in graph_store.py, 16 in vector_store.py and 4 in the much smaller core/tables.py — including on `register()`, which raises two bespoke exception types and documents neither under a `Raises:` heading. Its prose is also unusual in kind: long stretches of inline comment are written as commit-message narration in past tense ("Measured before this existed…", "A SEPARATE try/except, on purpose"), which records history well but repeatedly asserts guarantees the surrounding code does not actually provide (findings 2, 3, 4, 5). Typing is the weakest axis in both files: `Any | None` for the graph handle, `-> Any` on `_query`, `**legacy: Any` on `register`, and an entirely unannotated module-level `__getattr__` mean roughly thirty row-unpacking sites in `load()` and `_load_tables_sync` are unchecked by a strict mypy that pyproject configures but CI never runs.

**periphery** — The area is broadly consistent with the house contract on the mechanical points: all 12 non-`__init__` modules carry the `# GraphRAG SDK — <Area>: <Thing>` header, no line exceeds the 100-char ruff limit, and `from __future__ import annotations` is present everywhere except `utils/cypher.py` (finding periphery-25). Docstring quality is bimodal: the two ABCs (`chunking_strategies/base.py`, `loaders/base.py`) and four of five chunker class docstrings are exemplary Google style with `Args:` and `Example::` blocks — `sentence_token_cap.py`'s 30-line justification of its 384-token default is the best documentation in the repo — but the concrete `chunk()`/`load()` overrides carry no docstring at all (0 of 5 chunkers, 0 of 3 loaders), two of three loaders leave their `encoding` constructor parameter undocumented (periphery-30), and no loader docstring mentions what it raises even though all three raise (periphery-29). Logging convention has split in two: the loaders use lazy `%`-args like the rest of the codebase while `tracer.py` and `graph_viz.py` use eager f-strings (periphery-24). Typing is the weakest axis — `int | object` sentinel unions in `structural_chunking.py`, a bare `Any` connection in `graph_viz.py`, an unannotated closure parameter in `markdown_loader.py` and an unnarrowable Optional dereference in `contextual_chunking.py` are four distinct strict-mypy failures in six files, exactly the latent debt the uncheck-in-CI setup predicts. On the security question specifically: I traced all ~45 backtick interpolation sites and every one routes through `sanitize_cypher_label` except `graph_store.py:134`, which is a genuine gap (periphery-1) currently gated only by mapping-layer identifier validation.

**pipeline** — The three source modules are mostly disciplined: `pipeline.py`, `lexical_graph.py` and `ingestion/__init__.py` all carry the `# GraphRAG SDK — <Area>: <Thing>` banner (with `# Pattern:` / `# Origin:` lines on `pipeline.py`), all four files have `from __future__ import annotations` where applicable, and `pipeline.py` / `backfill.py` both declare `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)`; `backfill.py` is the lone ingestion module that opens with a docstring instead of the banner. Docstring format is the weakest axis: `IngestionPipeline.__init__` and `run` use full Google `Args:`/`Returns:` (plus an `Example::` block), but six of the nine remaining functions across the three modules — `_prune`, `_filter_quality`, `_remap_mentions`, `_build_lexical_graph`, `_mark_content_hash`, `_write_mentions` — give prose and an untagged "Returns" sentence with no `Args:` section at all, and `BackfillExecutor.run` documents five required keyword arguments by pointing at the module docstring rather than listing them; no docstring in any of the four files has a `Raises:` section even though `run` raises `ValueError` and `IngestionError` and the backfill layer defines its own exception. Sphinx roles are also split between siblings: `backfill.py` uses `:py:class:` / `:py:attr:` throughout while `pipeline.py` and `lexical_graph.py` use only double-backtick literals. Typing is complete on every signature, but `Any` appears six times across the targets, twice where a real type is already imported in the same file (`EvolutionResult.ontology`) or in a sibling module at the same layer (`graph_store`, typed `GraphStore` in `backfill.py` and `Any` in `pipeline.py`), and logging style mixes four lazy %-format calls with two f-string calls inside `pipeline.py` alone.

**providers** — The five modules mostly honour the house contract on the surface: four of the six files carry the `# GraphRAG SDK — <Area>: <Thing>` header, all five `.py` modules have `from __future__ import annotations`, and every module except `_timeout.py` declares `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)`. Where the area falls down is docstring discipline and factoring. Documentation is inverted relative to visibility: the private helpers `_raw_embed_sync`, `_raw_embed_async`, `_build_create_kwargs`, `_is_reasoning_model`, `summarize_exception` and `is_transient_embedding_error` all get proper Google-style or rationale docstrings, while roughly twelve public methods across the four provider classes (`invoke`, `ainvoke`, `embed_query`, `embed_documents`, `aembed_query`, `aembed_documents` in both `litellm.py` and `openrouter.py`) have none at all, and `_timeout.py`'s two functions have neither a module header nor any docstring — against `base.abatch_invoke` and `base.ainvoke_messages`, which show the Args/Returns house form correctly. Only about a third of the docstrings that do exist carry `Args:`/`Returns:` sections; `Raises:` appears nowhere in the area despite `LLMTimeoutError`/`EmbeddingTimeoutError` being raised from six places. Typing is the weakest axis: five factory methods have no return annotation, four cached-client attributes are unannotated (guaranteed strict-mypy assignment errors), and `wait_for_provider_call` is `Any -> Any`, erasing the response type at all nine call sites — real latent debt since mypy is configured strict but never run in CI. The two provider modules have also drifted apart from each other: `openrouter.py` centralises kwarg assembly in one `_build_create_kwargs` while `litellm.py` keeps two hand-copied versions, and the same 35-line retry loop is duplicated four times between them plus a fifth, behaviourally divergent copy in `base.py` — the divergence that produced the missing-jitter bug.

**resolution-base** — Against the house contract these three files are mostly compliant at the surface: all three carry the "# GraphRAG SDK — <Area>: <Thing>" header (base.py adds a "# Pattern:" line, exact_match.py an "# Origin:" line), base.py and exact_match.py both have "from __future__ import annotations" and module-level "logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)", both gate LLMInterface behind TYPE_CHECKING, and the public entry points are async. Docstring format is where the divergence is real rather than cosmetic: exactly one docstring in base.py uses the Google Args:/Returns: sections the contract calls for (ResolutionStrategy.resolve, line 668), while the five module-level helpers — exact_match_merge, description_list, set_merged_descriptions, merge_source_ids, flatten_remap, remap_relationships — are prose-only, and exact_match_merge's 30-line prose block leaves three of its nine parameters (label_gate, cross_label_vote, rejected_out) entirely undocumented and asserts a single-LLM-batch cost that cross_label_vote falsifies; ExactMatchResolution.resolve has no docstring at all despite overriding one that does. Sphinx roles (:class:, :func:, :data:) and an Example:: block are used well in exact_match.py's class docstring and in the ABC, so the better pattern exists in-tree and is simply not applied consistently. Typing is the other gap: four bare generics (base.py:45, 188, 212, 333) sit alongside fully parameterised siblings in the same function, and since mypy is strict in pyproject but not run in CI they are silent debt that also removes all static checking from the heterogeneous sl_entries/cl_candidates dicts that drive Stages 1-6. The recurring substantive theme is the one the codebase already documents about itself — a second copy of a merge loop: Stage 1 and Stage 6 re-implement merge_source_ids inline (and diverge from it), Stage 7 re-implements flatten_remap verbatim, and Stage 6's merged_labels handling is a partial copy of llm_verified_resolution.py:1238-1252 that lost the "labels must travel too" rule.

**resolution-llm-a** — The module follows the house contract at the file level: the "# GraphRAG SDK — Ingestion: LLM-Verified Resolution" header, `from __future__ import annotations`, `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)`, and full type hints on 1 of 1 dataclass and on every signature and nested closure except `_encoding()` (line 336), which is the module's single missing return annotation and a real mypy --strict violation given that CI does not run mypy. Docstring coverage is high but the format is not uniform: `labels_compatible`, `_pair_distance`, `_verify_batched` and `_cross_label_merge` follow the Google shape with Sphinx roles and (in two cases) a `Returns:` section, while `differ_only_in_digits` opens with examples instead of a summary line and carries no Args/Returns despite a non-obvious boolean polarity, `_pack` and `_ask_windows` omit `Returns:` for non-obvious tuple/dict shapes, and `_encoding` and `_count_tokens` have no docstring at all. Where docstrings exist they are unusually rich in measurement provenance, which is a genuine strength, but two of them contradict the code they sit on (`_cross_label_merge`'s documented `llm_confirmed_merge_count`, `_parse_batch_verdicts`' fallback rule) and one inline comment asserts a guarantee the code does not provide (ask-pairs "only reaches the LLM"). The largest style/maintenance problem is structural rather than cosmetic: three substantial blocks — the ~35-line survivor merge loop, the ~45-line cross-label second vote, and the Union-Find plus `cluster_families` bookkeeping — exist as verbatim copies in `_cross_label_merge` and `_embedding_and_llm_merge`, which is precisely the duplication that `ExactMatchResolution`'s own docstring names as the historic cause of three separate merge bugs in this codebase.

**resolution-llm-b** — The module gets the structural house rules right: the `# GraphRAG SDK — Ingestion: LLM-Verified Resolution` header, `from __future__ import annotations`, `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)`, full Pydantic-free dataclass typing on `_VerificationRequest`, and an unusually thorough class docstring that documents all 21 constructor parameters in a proper Args: block with measurement tables and Sphinx `:data:`/`:class:` roles. Below the class docstring the contract breaks down: not one of the 16 module-level functions and methods has an Args: section, only `_cross_label_merge` and `_embedding_and_llm_merge` have a Returns:, and `_encoding` (line 336) has neither a docstring nor a return annotation — the single strict-mypy annotation hole in 2060 lines, where the named sibling `exact_match_merge` in base.py:148 does supply an explicit Returns:. Comment density is the file's real style problem: several explanations run 15-25 lines and the same measured rationale is transcribed into two places (the survivor merge loop's "Both run BEFORE the copy-keys loop" block appears verbatim at lines 1213 and 2005), which is exactly the drift that this codebase's own `exact_match.py` docstring warns produced three past defects — and the two copies have already diverged by one paragraph. Prompt-construction style is inconsistent in a way that has behavioural consequences: `_cross_label_merge` defines a correct `_desc` helper (line 863) while the four sibling call sites in `_embedding_and_llm_merge` use the broken `props.get("description", "(no description)")` idiom, so the same module formats the same field two different ways.

**retrieval** — The retrieval package is mostly faithful to the house contract: all 11 modules carry the `# GraphRAG SDK — Retrieval: <Thing>` header, all have `from __future__ import annotations`, and all but the two reranking modules declare `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)` (cosine.py declares one and never uses it). Where it drifts, it drifts in two consistent directions. First, docstring shape: `filter_facts_by_relevance`, `RetrievalStrategy.search` and `RerankingStrategy.rerank` are proper Google-style with Args/Returns and `Example::` blocks, but four of the five public module-level coroutines (`search_relates_edges`, `discover_entities`, `retrieve_chunks`, `expand_relationships`) have a `Returns:` block with no `Args:`, none of them document the `LatencyBudgetExceededError` passthrough they all implement, and the only concrete reranker's `rerank` has no docstring at all. Second, typing: the package annotates carefully in most places yet leaves at least six strict-mypy violations behind — `kwargs: dict`, `def __getattr__(name: str):`, `seen: set[tuple]`, `items = []`, `_format(...) -> Any` in local.py against `-> RetrieverResult` in multi_path.py, and `condition: Any` / `route() -> Any` in router.py — and `Any` is used for the graph and vector stores throughout rather than a Protocol. Line length and import ordering look ruff-clean throughout. The sharpest consistency problem is not cosmetic: the entity-id derivation now exists in four places (one canonical `compute_entity_id` plus three hand-inlined and subtly different copies in retrieval), and cosine similarity in two, which is the duplication class this codebase has already been bitten by.

**structured** — The four files are unusually well documented by any normal standard — nearly every function carries a multi-paragraph Google-style docstring that names the concrete bug it fixes, and the house header comment, `from __future__ import annotations` and module `logger` are present in three of the four modules. The gaps are narrow and specific: `record_loader.py` is the one module with no module-level logger (its three sibling loaders all have one) and the one with an undocumented method (`_sniff`); `_reference_key` (structured_pipeline.py:186) and `EdgeMapping.columns` (mapping.py:234) are the only members of their respective peer groups with no docstring at all, against 10 and 3 documented siblings; and `profile_columns` (mapping_proposal.py:110) is a public cross-module function with a one-line docstring that contradicts all three of its call sites. Type hints are complete on every signature and return, with the single systematic exception of `graph_store: Any`, which appears at every store boundary in this area and is what lets an unchecked `int` return value (finding structured-1) pass unnoticed under a config that claims mypy --strict. The more serious consistency problem is not formatting but duplication: three copies of `typed_properties`, three spellings of "basename of the source", and three validation guards in `_normalise` that restate `TableMapping.__post_init__` word for word — the exact bug class the repo's history already records once.

**vector-identity** — identity.py is the stronger of the two files against the house contract: module header, `from __future__ import annotations`, module-level logger, full type hints, and unusually good Sphinx-role docstrings that record *why* each rule exists (`canonical_key`, `_merge_core`, `_fold`). Its only real style gaps are one function with no docstring at all (`_tokens`, line 182, between `_fold` and `_core` which both have one), a constant named `_MAX_PER_LABEL` that actually bounds a blocking-key block and is defined at line 370 below the function whose docstring at line 332 explains it, and a docstring claim of linear cost that the quadratic inner loop at line 413 does not honour. vector_store.py keeps the header, future import and logger, but its docstrings are inconsistent: 3 of 5 search methods carry `Args:`/`Returns:` and `search_entities` carries neither, `backfill_entity_embeddings` documents behaviour the code does not implement, and the `drop_*` trio diverges from the `create_*` trio in return type. Typing is the weaker axis in vector_store.py — three `Any`s where a real type exists and one unannotated empty list that mypy --strict (configured but not run in CI) would reject — and error handling is uniformly `except Exception` with a warning and an empty return, which is consistent within the file but erases the difference between a misconfigured index and an empty graph.

**x-async** — The package is unusually disciplined about the documented house style: 74 of 77 modules open with the `# GraphRAG SDK — <Area>: <Thing>` header (only `ingestion/backfill.py` and `storage/ontology_store.py` lead with a module docstring instead, and `core/providers/_timeout.py` has no header at all), and `from __future__ import annotations` is present in all 61 non-`__init__` modules except `utils/cypher.py`. Docstrings are the strongest part of the codebase — Google-style with Args/Returns/Raises, Sphinx roles and `Example::` blocks, and unusually good "here is the measurement behind this constant" prose — but that quality is what makes the drift visible: `core/providers/_timeout.py` has neither a module header, a `logger`, nor a docstring on either of its two public functions, while every sibling in `core/providers/` has all three. The sharper inconsistencies are behavioural rather than cosmetic, and almost always have a correct sibling a few files away: the loaders uniformly offload blocking work with `await asyncio.to_thread(self._load_sync, ...)` while `CsvRecordLoader.load_records` and all three tiktoken chunkers do the same class of work straight on the event loop; `GLiNERExtractor` and `SpacyExtractor` both guard their model cache with a double-checked `ClassVar[threading.Lock]` while `FastCorefResolver._load_model` guards nothing; `CompositeExtractor` handles `gather(return_exceptions=True)` results with `isinstance(result, BaseException)` while `GraphExtraction` checks only `Exception`; and `backfill.py` documents a bounded worker-pool precisely because "50k pending tasks up front" is bad, while `discovery/pipeline.py` gathers the entire source list unbounded. Typing is thorough on signatures, but the duck-typed `Any` on `IngestionPipeline.graph_store` / `vector_store` and on `OpenRouterLLM._get_client()`'s return leave whole call paths unchecked under the strict-but-unrun mypy config.

**x-docs** — The prose docs are well written but have drifted badly from the source, and the drift is systematic rather than scattered. Three whole migrations that CHANGELOG.md itself documents were never applied to the guides: the v1.0.1 removal of `rag.graph_store`/`rag.vector_store` (13 live snippets across 5 files still call them), the v1.0.1 switch of `finalize()` from a dict to a `FinalizeResult` model (3 files), and the v1.2 GraphSchema-to-Ontology rename (9 files, ~60 occurrences of `GraphSchema`/`EntityType`/`RelationType`/`PropertyType` plus the deprecated `schema=` kwarg). The source itself is disciplined about the house style: the `# GraphRAG SDK - <Area>: <Thing>` header, `from __future__ import annotations`, module-level `logger`, and Google docstrings with `Example::` blocks and Sphinx roles are present in every strategy ABC and throughout api/main.py, and the docstrings are consistently more accurate than the .mdx pages describing the same functions (e.g. `completion`'s docstring at api/main.py:4258 contradicts docs/api-reference.mdx:149, and `ExactMatchResolution`'s docstring explicitly names the stale behaviour docs/ingestion.mdx:170 still teaches). docs/structured-ingestion.mdx, docs/ontology-discovery.mdx and docs/ontology-evolution.mdx are the exceptions: written recently, they use `Ontology`/`Entity`/`Relation` and the current facade methods correctly and are the model the older pages should be brought to. The most damaging single line is docs/api-reference.mdx:62, which asserts `graph_store`/`vector_store` are public attributes and is the apparent source of every downstream broken snippet.

**x-duplication** — The tree is unusually disciplined on the mechanical parts of the house style: all 61 non-`__init__` modules carry `from __future__ import annotations` except `utils/cypher.py`, module-level `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)` is essentially universal, the `# GraphRAG SDK — <Area>: <Thing>` header is present on every module I read except `ingestion/backfill.py` (which opens with a module docstring instead), and the top-level `__all__` is exactly consistent with what `__init__.py` imports. Docstrings are the best-argued I have seen in a codebase of this size — `canonical_key`, `normalize_entity_name`, `flatten_remap` and `SentenceTokenCapChunking.max_tokens` all cite measured numbers for why the rule is what it is — but the quality is bimodal: `retrieval/strategies/result_assembly.py` and `retrieval/reranking_strategies/cosine.py` use bare one-liners with no Args/Returns sections, next to siblings in the same package that use full Google-style blocks with Sphinx roles, and several docstrings are now stale or contradicted by the code (`ChunkContext`'s "snapshot of the ontology" that nothing reads, `merge_source_ids`'s "Stage 1 and Stage 5" when the second copy is Stage 6, `is_acronym_of`'s "this is the only rule that can see AIHS"). Two style breaks recur without explanation: function-local imports in `api/main.py:1503` and `retrieval/strategies/local.py:95` where the module already imports at file scope, and five cross-module imports of underscore-private names. The dominant defect by far is duplication rather than style — the documented "separate copy of the merge loop" bug class is still live in at least six shapes (cluster-merge apply loop ×2, merged_labels fold ×3, remap chain-walk ×4, union-find ×3, source_chunk_ids union ×4, per-type value coercion ×2), and in four of those the copies have already drifted into behavioural disagreement.

**x-errors** — Surface-level house style is followed almost everywhere: 74 of the 77 modules open with the `# GraphRAG SDK — <Area>: <Thing>` header (the two exceptions, `ingestion/backfill.py` and `storage/ontology_store.py`, open with a module docstring instead; only `core/providers/_timeout.py` has neither), every non-`__init__` module except `utils/cypher.py` carries `from __future__ import annotations`, and `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)` is present in every module that logs. Google-style docstrings are the norm and the best files go well beyond it — `FalkorDBConnection.query`, `VectorStore.ensure_indices` and `EntityDeduplicator._merge` all document `Args:`/`Returns:`/`Raises:` and explain *why* — but `Raises:` is the weak spot: `FalkorDBConnection.query` documents it while `GraphStore.upsert_nodes`, which raises `DatabaseError` from its per-item fallback, does not, and the five `VectorStore` search methods document a `Returns:` their `except Exception: return []` handlers contradict. Typing discipline is where the gap between the pyproject contract and reality is widest: mypy is configured `strict = true` but CI runs ruff only, and the package carries 8 genuinely untyped defs, 19 `# type: ignore` comments (5 of them the same masked `raise last_exc` Optional violation, 3 of them `no-untyped-def` ignores on continuation lines where mypy does not honour them), 41 `**kwargs: Any` on public signatures, 22 `-> Any` returns, and at least 10 bare `dict`/`list` generics that `disallow_any_generics` rejects. Exception discipline is the most inconsistent area of all: only 31 raise sites use the `core/exceptions.py` hierarchy against roughly 113 bare `ValueError`/`RuntimeError`/`TypeError`, five hierarchy classes are never raised or caught at all, and six public exception types — five of them exported in `__all__` alongside `GraphRAGError` — inherit `RuntimeError` or `ValueError` instead of it.

**x-security** — The house style is followed closely across this surface: all 60 non-`__init__.py` modules except `utils/cypher.py` carry `from __future__ import annotations`, every module that logs declares `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)`, and the module headers use the `# GraphRAG SDK — <Area>: <Thing>` form consistently. Docstrings are unusually rich — the graph_store, deduplicator and llm_verified_resolution files use Google sections plus Sphinx roles and cite measured benchmark numbers — but the security-relevant contracts are the weakest part: `GraphStore.rename_node_property` (graph_store.py:1689) asserts a character-class guarantee that `sanitize_cypher_label` (utils/cypher.py:22) does not implement, and `GraphStore`'s class docstring at line 75 claims "All operations use parameterised queries to prevent injection" while roughly 25 methods interpolate labels and property names by f-string. Error-logging style is split in the same way: `core/providers/_retry.py` states and enforces a sanitized-WARNING policy via `summarize_exception`, yet five embedding call sites in `storage/vector_store.py` and `ingestion/resolution_strategies/llm_verified_resolution.py` still use `logger.warning(f"...: {exc}")` with the raw provider string. Prompt hygiene is likewise inconsistent — `api/main.py` delimits and neutralizes untrusted retrieved text before the answering call, while the resolution prompts interpolate the same class of untrusted text with no guard at all.

**x-style** — The house style contract is followed far more consistently than the brief implies, and several of the divergences it asks about simply do not exist in this tree — worth reporting as much as the real gaps. Naming is uniform: 77/77 files use absolute `graphrag_sdk.*` imports with zero relative imports; `ctx: Context` appears 77 times and `context: Context` zero times; `Optional[X]` appears zero times against 400+ `X | None`; and all 42 modules that import `logging` declare exactly `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)` at module level, with no exceptions. Module headers are 74/77 compliant and `from __future__ import annotations` is 61/77 (the 16 misses being 15 `__init__.py` files plus `utils/cypher.py`). Where the codebase diverges, it diverges on documentation rather than on layout: 121 public definitions out of 929 total have no docstring (226 including private ones, ~76% coverage), and the pattern is systematic — every strategy ABC carries a full Google `Args:`/`Returns:` block while all 20 concrete overrides of those methods carry nothing, and the four provider classes users instantiate most document 0 of 25 constructor parameters. `Raises:` is the weakest section by far: 13 sections against 224 `raise <X>Error(` sites, with `api/main.py` at 3-of-71 and `ingestion/mapping.py` at 0-of-13. Two smaller flavour splits run through the docs — `:py:class:` (57 uses, 7 files) versus `:class:` (90 uses, 17 files), with three files mixing both, and Pydantic v1 `class Config` surviving on the root `DataModel` while all 8 other model configs use v2 `model_config`. Typing debt is real but bounded: 11 missing return annotations and 22 signatures where `Any` stands in for `GraphStore`/`VectorStore`/`FalkorDBConnection`/`Embedder`, all of which `cached_chunk_extraction.py` already shows how to fix with a `TYPE_CHECKING` import; none of it is caught today because mypy is configured strict but not run in CI.

**x-tests** — The suite is, on the whole, unusually disciplined for its size: 59 of 63 test modules carry `from __future__ import annotations`, 61 of 63 open with a module docstring that states the invariant the file pins, and the strongest files (test_llm_verified_resolution.py, test_structured_data_safety.py, test_signed_properties.py, test_identity.py, test_deduplicator_queries.py) write assertion messages that name the production regression and the measurement behind it — a convention that makes a failure self-explanatory. Test docstrings are deliberately prose rather than Google-style Args/Returns, which is right for tests and consistent across the tree. Divergence clusters in the oldest files: test_facade.py, test_pipeline.py, test_integration.py, test_exceptions.py, test_circuit_breaker.py and test_router.py contain the suite's whole population of near-vacuous assertions (`assert result is not None` x4, `>= 0` x3, `isinstance(...)` as the sole check, one `pytest.raises(Exception)`), while newer files in the same areas assert exact values. Type-hint coverage is the weakest axis: the shared fakes in conftest.py and the four duplicated embedder doubles in test_resolver_dedup.py/test_integration.py annotate inconsistently (`**kwargs` untyped in all of them, `MockLLM.ainvoke_messages` untyped entirely), so mypy --strict debt is concentrated in exactly the helpers every other test depends on. Four helper classes doing the same orthogonal/near-vector job across two files is the only real duplication I found, and it carries a latent aliasing bug in two of the four copies.
